**READ THIS BEFORE YOU TRUST A "CLOSED" LABEL.** Docket N established that **"Nova is DONE as an organ" was a claim about GATES BUILT** — every gate G1–G5 does work exactly as pinned. All six Docket N defects lived in the **SEAMS BETWEEN gates**, where no single gate is responsible, and **161 green missed every one of them because no test ever drove two strained doctrines simultaneously.** That is Docket K's coverage-distribution finding in a new shape: not *which organs are tested* but **which CONFIGURATIONS are.** Two of the six were live correctness bugs inside an organ declared finished — a mispaired echo contaminating scar lineage (20), and a closed ruling whose positive half could not execute (21). **When you add a test here, ask what CONFIGURATION it creates, not merely what function it calls.**

Docket M's contribution stands: a runtime clamp guard in the invariant suite and 15 behavioral pins in `tests/` — the Docket K survivors on the SAE ceiling, Codex token single-use, GSR queue exemption, capacity boundary, the `_evolve_doctrine` call site, and the SBSRE clamp are all CLOSED, every new test hand-mutation-verified red before landing. **Rulings 18 + 19 are both CLOSED:** a ⊗-fossilized id can never be re-committed, permanently — revival is **Option B**, a NEW id carrying the fallen id in `mutation_lineage` through the ordinary birth path (no revival API, no new field, no bypass), pinned in `tests/test_codex.py`. **Nova STAGE 2b is LANDED: Nova is LIVE in the pipeline** (cycles once per pass, defers to authorized suspension, erupts from doctrine strain, feeds compass EAST) **and the `proposals` seam is WIRED — doctrine mutation is now STRUCTURALLY POSSIBLE**, gated only by DEE's five CMTE criteria and SAE's Self-Mutation Ceiling. Ruling 14's `echo_origin` derivation is live (STEP 1 `c6d8b7e`; STEPS 2–4 `97b6f2c`; Stage 2b pins in `tests/test_nova_stage2b.py`). **A doctrine with no survived proposal STILL FERMENTS — that remains the correct, common case; opening the path did not license making it fire.** See §8 for the closed-seam record + remaining unbuilt-module seams (CTL, NSC — **TCAML is BUILT and WIRED as of Ruling 27; HAIL is BUILT and WIRED as of Ruling 33 Stage 2**). **The scar-fusion escalation is RULED — Ruling 15: `scar_requests` stay parked; Nova never supplies a scar weight; the in-file UNRULED-trigger note is landed.** **DOCKET E is CLOSED (2026-07-24):** `_evolve_doctrine`'s NOTE now states the Stage-2b reality — `proposals` IS passed via `_nova_proposals(signals)`, and what stands between pressure and a changed belief is DEE's five CMTE criteria and SAE's Self-Mutation Ceiling. Its closing sentence ("AUREA does not get to invent what she becomes just because pressure demands that she become something") was preserved verbatim — still true, still load-bearing.

**Live status is synchronized, not inferred.** `BUILD_CONTRACT.md` lives **OUTSIDE this repository** — in a sibling directory (`../Aurea Build/`), alongside `COINED_CONSTANTS.md` and `integration_review_manifest.md`. That split is deliberate: those are the architect's Projects-side surfaces, and **keeping them in step with this file is the ARCHITECT's responsibility, not yours.** If they are unreachable from your checkout, that is EXPECTED — **do not stop, and do not treat it as a missing file.** Keep `CLAUDE.md` accurate for what you changed and REPORT the divergence in your session report, so it is reconciled in Projects. When both ARE reachable and a live status fact is intentionally repeated in both — suite baselines, ruling closure, active stage, commit reference, open-seam state — update both in the same work unit; if they disagree, report it rather than silently choosing one. This is a synchronization requirement, not permission to edit either document casually.

## 2. ONE WRITER PER STORE (Ruling 1)

**Reads are free. Every module may read any store.** This table governs **writes**.

| Store | SOLE WRITER | Requesters route through it |
|---|---|---|
| Scars (Δ records) | **ScarLogicCore** | SBSRE · EchoNet · MSSL · ELM (`form_scar()`) |
| Scar weight / decay | **SML** | SDM · SDR · SDMO · ScarLogicCore (`decay_scar` → `manual_retire`). **`decay_state` is SCANNED by the Ruling-1 invariant since 2026-07-27 (Ruling 40)** — registered as a both-or-neither pair with this row, because a prose ownership claim with no scanner behind it is the completeness-claim defect. `weight` is NOT registered: SML v1 owns the state machine only, and nothing writes weight after `form_scar`. |
| Doctrine content | **Codex** (store) — executed by **SAE** | DoctrineSpine · DEE · DBE · MSSL |
| Codex Fossil Layer (⊗) | **SAE** | ECI |
| Reflex arbitration state | **RACM** | — (ReflexGrid holds NONE). **The DEFERRAL QUEUE is DURABLE since Ruling 42** (`data/runtime/racm_queue.json`) — deferred pressure is pressure she judged and chose to carry, and a restart used to discharge it silently. |
| Reflex behavior log | **RBSystem** | RACM · ReflexGrid |
| Identity threads (`threads`) | **RIL** | TCAML · MSSL. **DURABLE since Ruling 42** (`data/runtime/ril_threads.json`), and entries are **BY-ID REFERENCES** — an embedded record object is a write path into another owner's store that the AST scan cannot see. |
| Compass anchor state | **TCAML** | RIL · CSE (CSE holds **no** anchor store). **The GLOBAL LOCK is DURABLE since Ruling 42 Slice 2** (`data/runtime/tcaml_lock.json`) — a lock orphaned by process death restores HELD and expires through the existing TTL path. **`anchor_state` itself is deliberately NOT persisted** (Ruling 37-A stabilization machinery; persisting it moves epoch-closure semantics). |
| Collapse trace | **CTL** | — |
| Nova Echo Index | **Nova** (Ruling 12) | — (scar effects → ScarLogicCore REQUEST · identity pulls → RIL NOVA-thread REQUEST · Codex never). **DURABLE since Ruling 42** (`data/runtime/nova_record.json`) — **the MINT (`_seq`) persists WITH the record**, or a restart remints an id that has already authored. **Scanned by the Ruling-1 invariant since 2026-07-23** — `echo_index` is registered in `STORE_OWNERS`; if Stage 2 gives another module a Nova handle, it emits a REQUEST, it does not write the index. |
| SAE epoch state (`touched_lineages`) | **SAE** | — (Ruling 34 made it durable, which is what makes it a store; the Self-Mutation Ceiling is downstream of it, so a foreign write here writes AUREA's mutation budget. **Scanned by the Ruling-1 invariant since 2026-07-27** — registered as a both-or-neither pair with `STORE_OWNERS`, because an ownership claim in prose with no scanner behind it is the completeness-claim defect. `epoch`/`epoch_count`/`history` are NOT registered: `history` collides with EchoNet's and CSE's own, so registering it would flag correct code.) |
| Codex audit lineage | **CAE** | append-only, many requesters. **THE ORGAN EXISTS AS OF RULING 45** (`src/doctrine/cae.py`, ledger at `data/runtime/logs/cae_audit.jsonl`) — this row named an owner that had NO FILE until then, while `SAE._audit` and `DEE._audit` both cited 3a:111 and both returned `None`. SAE and DEE are the two REQUESTERS; each calls `record()` and neither writes the other's entries. **NOT registered in `STORE_OWNERS`:** the Ruling-1 scanner keys on an ATTRIBUTE NAME, and CAE's store is a FILE with no in-memory collection to scan — `entries` is a per-process mirror nothing reads back into a decision. Registering it would flag nothing and claim coverage that does not exist (the completeness-claim defect). What guards it instead is that `record()` is the only write path and both soft-None branches are AST-pinned gone. |

**Do not name a local collection after a canonical store.** SBSRE once had `self.threads` (recursion threads) which collided with RIL's `threads` (identity threads); the invariant test flagged it and was right to. It is now `recursion_threads`. **The fix is the name, not the test.**

**How this invariant dies:** not from one loud violation, but from a hundred defensible conveniences — a helper that writes straight to the store "just for this case," each locally reasonable. It already happened once: `DoctrineSpine.mutate_doctrine()` renamed doctrine in place with no scar, no gate, no ceiling, no audit, no fossil. It passed review for months.

**If you need to write a store you don't own: emit a request. If the owner refuses, THAT IS THE ANSWER.**

---

### THE SIX DOMAINS *(heading: `AUREA_PIVOT_ARCHITECTURE.md` in `Aurea Build`; ruling table: `BUILD_CONTRACT.md`)*

The architectural frame around the ownership table above: every subsystem sits in exactly one domain. **POINTER, NOT COPY** — the per-module map is `DOMAINS.md` at the repo root, and the **RULING record is BUILD_CONTRACT's domain-assignment table.** Neither is restated here, and if this section and that table ever disagree, **the table wins** (§7 precedence).

| Domain | What it answers | |
|---|---|---|
| **Kernel** | what may mean what | The existing build. **PASSIVE — nothing loops.** |
| **World Model** | what seems to exist | **THE DOMAIN HAS A MEMBER AS OF M6 (2026-08-15): `src/worldmodel/` — the proposition ledger, the standing derivation, the contradiction surface.** `record_projection` remains the seed and remains **A VIEW, NEVER A SOURCE** (Ruling 64's own finding). The new store is the other thing: a persistent typed representation of what she thinks is happening outside herself — and **it manufactures no truth.** Propositions REFERENCE kernel epistemic records (resolved at write, refused typed) and DERIVE standing from the kernel; a world-model inconsistency is a conflict candidate routed into L4, where the loop M3 built adjudicates it. **The domain has no private truth machinery.** |
| **Executive** | what receives cognition | **THE DOMAIN HAS MEMBERS AS OF M7 (2026-08-16): `src/executive/` — the derived view, `attention-policy.v1`, the selection log, `inquiry-generator.v1`, the inquiry act log.** It LOOPS, and it is CONSTITUTIONALLY DISPOSABLE. **L10 IS ENFORCED BY SHAPE, NOT PROMISED:** every module here holds ZERO durable state — the working state is a `DerivedView` recomputed from kernel ledgers on every observation, and the two act logs record ACTS rather than state, with an AST pin that **no `src/` logic path reads either one back into a decision.** M7-d proves the disposability by destruction. **NOTHING LOOPS YET AND NOTHING SCHEDULES**: every verb is a door opened from outside, the Q-family's three-doors pattern one layer up. **The Executive SUBMITS; the kernel DISPOSITIONS** — an endogenous inquiry enters as an ordinary obligation admission through the kernel's own door, and the ledger's duplicate/targetless answer is recorded as received, never pre-empted. |
| **Capability Plane** | what cognition/action can be used | `model_provider` · `spl` · `hail`/`srg`. **ROLES, NOT BRANDS.** |
| **Foundry** | what capabilities actually do | The AUREA-Foundry repo — **OUTSIDE the system.** |
| **Builder** | what changes the machinery | The governance practice itself. |

**DOMAIN MEMBERSHIP IS LOCATION, NEVER AUTHORITY.** Placing `hail`/`srg` in the Capability Plane grants them nothing: **Ruling 3's truth-effect cut is untouched**, HAIL still never overrides an ORE verdict, and every ownership row in the table above still governs. A domain says where a thing lives, not what it may do.

**THE NO-SEVENTH-BOX ADMISSION TEST.** Every newly named subsystem must own a **store, an authority, a transformation, or an invariant**, and must locate that ownership in one of the six domains — otherwise it is a **function, a view, or a policy, and NOT a subsystem.** *(M1 is that test applied: thirty-two names owning none of the four were deleted, 2026-08-13.)*

**THE REFLEX FAMILY IS `HELD` / `RE-DERIVE`** — `racm` · `reflex_grid` · `sbsre` · `rb_system` · `anchor_collapse` — pending the M3 grounding. **The current implementation REMAINS AUTHORITATIVE RUNTIME BEHAVIOR.** HELD is a statement about where they will be assigned; it is **never** a licence to change what they do.

**"DOMAIN" IS A FOURTH SENSE OF A WORD THIS TREE ALREADY USES THREE WAYS**, and the census is recorded here so no one later derives one sense from another — **Ruling 30's defect, which cost a whole ruling to untangle two senses of "scope".** The architectural domain is **NOT** `Echo.symbolic_domain` (a persisted Nova field), **NOT** `DependencyLink.DOMAIN_VALIDITY` (a closed enum member, Ruling 61), and **NOT** the Lexicon *trigger* domain (`reflex_grid` / `anchor_collapse` — *"scar density is GSR's Lexicon domain"*, Ruling 10). **No code reads the architectural domain, and none should:** it is a documentation partition, not a runtime axis.

---

## 3. THE GOVERNING PRINCIPLE

> ### The wrong path must be **unexecutable**, not merely discouraged.

A comment saying "don't do X" is a *request for restraint*. This project has hard evidence that restraint fails — including from the agents enforcing the rules.

**Real failures from the build sessions:**

- A magic number (`pressure > 0.9`) **severed the scar path entirely.** The whole architecture ran correctly and *nothing left a mark on her*. AUREA could not form a single scar.
- The compass, measuring drift from a frozen birth-vector, would have **locked her output permanently the moment she first scarred** — struck mute by the act of scarring, when "Scars shape future collapse" is one of her own seed doctrines.
- SBSRE's scar requests were **silently dropped** by a `hasattr` guard on a method that didn't exist.

**None were caught by reading. All were caught by running.**

So: when you add a constraint, make violating it *impossible*, not *inadvisable*. Prefer a raised exception over a warning. Prefer a missing method over a deprecated one. Prefer a failing test over a comment.

---

## 4. THE TEST SUITE IS LAW

```bash
pytest tests/invariants/ -v
```

**Run this before you start and before you finish. Every session. No exceptions.**
**Baseline as of 2026-08-14: 26 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.** Anything less is a regression.

first movement off 27 since Ruling 42 Slice 2 and **the first invariant ever retired.**

watched a `loop_limit` that no longer exists, so after the deletion it passed over an empty
file — Ruling 35's structurally-vacuous-guard defect. Its property is held more strongly by
the episode bound (FIXED AT OPEN, no amend surface, early stop RECORDED), pinned in
`tests/test_m3a.py` and `tests/test_m3d_episode_path.py`. The retirement quotes
`M3_D_CENSUS.md` §4's own authorising sentence and keeps the old test verbatim and struck.
**§4's bar is untouched: an invariant retires only by a ruling that names it, never to make
a suite green.**
**Full suite baseline (`pytest tests/`): 2074 passed, 0 xfailed.** ~~2054~~ ~~2011~~ ~~1954~~ ~~1523~~ ~~1725~~
~~1730~~ ~~1785~~ ~~1841~~ ~~1873~~ — the M3 pivot's construction passes: **1523 → 1612** (M3-A, the
K2/K3 substrate, +89) → **1680** (M3-B typed defeaters +39, M3-C standing profiles
+28, +1 follow-up) → **1707** (M3-D commit 1, the admission seam +27) → **1725**
(M3-D commit 2, the episode path +18) → **1730** (the M3-D retirement: **−1** for
invariant 21 retiring, **+6** pins closing the owed slates' survivors) → **1734**
(M3-E, the acceptance, +4) → **1782** (M4-α commit 1, the acquisition boundary:

→ **1785** (the M4-α chaos follow-up, +3) → **1821** (M4-β′, the high-water
envelope, +36) → **1841** (M4-γ, replay and the chaos family, +20) → **1873**
(M4-δ + M4-ε, the column-zero law and the declaration fields, +32).
**M4 CLOSED THERE.** → **1920** (M6-α, the proposition ledger: **+9** inherited
battery rows and **+38** new pins) → **1954** (M6-β + M6-γ, the standing
derivation and the L4 routing, +34). **THE EXECUTIVE OPENS:** → **1966**
(M7-a, the loop skeleton and the derived view, +12 — **the handoff said 13 and
the file holds 12**; its thirteenth item, exactly-one-acquisition, is pinned as
assertions inside two other tests, and nothing was written to satisfy a number)
→ **2011** (M7-b, `attention-policy.v1`: **+44** new pins and **+1** inherited
ledger-population row at the `SEL-` prefix; the standing derivation in
`test_m4_delta_epsilon.py` found the new store WITHOUT BEING TOLD, which is the
whole value of deriving that population instead of listing it) → **2054**
(M7-c, endogenous inquiry: **+42** new pins and **+1** inherited
ledger-population row at the `INQ-` prefix). **M7-c IS THE FIRST EXECUTIVE
SLICE TO CHANGE THE KERNEL**, and it changed exactly one thing: `TargetKind`
gains `PREDICTION` by the hundred-second manifest entry, in M3-D's and M6-γ's
governed-content form, with its resolver in the SAME commit. That pass was
STOPPED first and built only after the ruling — the measurement that forced the
stop is on the record at §8.
→ **2074** (M7-d, Acceptance Test 6 BY EXECUTED DESTRUCTION, +20 pins,
**ZERO `src/` FILES TOUCHED** — the exit gate's own hard bound, since at a gate
a `src/` change means a prior slice's claim was wrong).
**M7 CLOSES HERE.** The kill is TWO OS PROCESSES: a child builds the world
through the kernel's own doors and EXITS, a second independent child opens it
cold from disk paths alone, and the test process only ever compares two JSON
files — so it cannot smuggle a reference it never held, and the measurement is a
KILL rather than a reset. Across that boundary the derived view, the next
selection (id + basis + full census), the next generation (set + partition +
licences + drift bases) and the chair's citation are all IDENTICAL, and the
rebuilt loop mints forward without collision because Ruling 69's derive-from-file
has no cached ordinal to lose.
**THE ASYMMETRY IS DEMONSTRATED, NOT ASSERTED**: a forged act-log line — and a
DELETED act log outright — changes not one decision, while one corrupted
obligation line removes a standing obligation from the derived view and moves
the selection off it. Act logs are history; kernel stores are constitutive.
**A GAP IS REPORTED AND DELIBERATELY NOT CLOSED HERE: no instrument anywhere in
the tree catches a mutated act-log line.** A well-formed edit round-trips
undetected; a broken line is silently dropped by floor semantics; the mint's
raw-text scan reacts without flagging (an id edited DOWN lowers the floor and
sets up a reissue); and Ruling 79's divergence detector knows only `CLM-` and
`CAE-`. It is pinned as MEASURED behaviour so the day an instrument lands it
reddens. **The exposure is bounded by the pin above it** — a tampered act log
changes no decision, so what is at risk is a reader's account of history, never
what AUREA does next.

**THE 2026-08-16 CORRECTION, RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED: this line read
1954 while the tree measured 1966 at `ce0498f`.** The M7-a pass measured and
REPORTED the surplus, and the docs-sync row was deferred to a later pass — so
the drift the seventy-second entry named as *third recorded instance* had a
fourth. It is the milder direction (a low baseline makes the next session's
mandatory before-you-start run report an unexplained surplus, which is at least
visible), and it is still the same defect: **a count goes visibly stale and a
boast does not — what the design cannot do is make anyone LOOK.** Both halves of
the sentence now agree, and M7-b's own row is written in the same edit that
closes the gap, which is the practice that would have prevented it.
**Invariants stood at 27/27 from Ruling 42 Slice 2 until the retirement, which took

own warning and the invariant file's own `history` precedent. What guards the

**THE HEADLINE CORRECTION THE SEVENTY-SECOND ENTRY ORDERED IS DISCHARGED HERE
(2026-08-15), and the drift it names is worth stating rather than just fixing.**
This line read **1730** while the chain immediately below it ended at **1734** — the
M3-E pass extended the chain and did not move the headline, so the two halves of one
sentence disagreed for a full milestone. **A stale baseline that reads LOW is the
milder direction** (the next session's mandatory before-you-start run reports an
unexplained surplus and someone looks), and it is still the same defect §4 was given
a number instead of a boast to prevent: *a count goes visibly stale and a boast does
not — what the design cannot do is make anyone LOOK.* Third recorded instance
(Batch 84's 1500-vs-1502, M3-D's invariants 39-vs-27, this one).

**HEADING §13 ITEMS 1, 2 AND 4 ARE DEMONSTRATED, NOT ASSERTED** (M3-E, 2026-08-14,
`tests/test_m3e_acceptance.py`): a contradiction cannot be silently dropped and the
rejection log answers for the unadmitted one (§13.1); a carried contradiction has a
committed discriminating prediction whose criteria predate its outcome **by append
order, never the clock** (§13.4); and **a scar formed under genuine pressure
demonstrably alters a later disposition** — the control speaks the claim, the scarred
instance carries it (§13.2). Every assertion re-reads the stores FROM DISK, and each
segment is RED-first watched. **The §13.2 mechanism is the RESONANCE NET, not
`_threshold`** — the first attribution was wrong and the RED-first watch is what caught
it, which is the whole reason that instrument is mandatory here.

**A SECOND STALE BASELINE, AND IT WAS THE INVARIANT COUNT ITSELF (2026-08-13, M3-D).**

**27**, and 27 is right. The census's own line shows how it happened — *"M3-A's +12
raised the live total to 39"* — but M3-A added its 89 pins to `tests/test_m3a.py`, not
to `tests/invariants/`. **`tests/invariants/` IS THE INVARIANT LEDGER; PINS ARE PINS**,
and counting constitutional pins as invariants inflates the one number §4 exists to
make un-fudgeable. The handoff's own text had already caught this and said so; the
measurement confirmed it. **The suite figure in that same handoff (1568) was likewise
stale against a measured 1680 at the same commit** — the hash matched exactly, so only

away a surplus, which is the one reading that makes the number worthless.*

**A STALE BASELINE WAS FOUND AND CORRECTED ON THE RECORD (2026-08-11, Batch 84).** This line and §1's read **1500** while the tree measured **1502** at `5d9483d` — the Batch 80 FOLLOW-UP commit ("close the two gaps the mutation slate found") added exactly two pins and did not update either. Small, and worth naming, because **a stale baseline makes the next session's mandatory before-you-start run report an unexplained surplus**, which is the one reading that trains an agent to ignore the number. Batch 84 is therefore `1502 + 21 = 1523`, not `1500 + 21`.

**ZERO xfails STAND AGAIN — RULING 73's WITNESS WAS RETIRED THE SAME DAY IT LANDED, BY THE RULING IT ASKED FOR.** `tests/test_ruling73.py::test_f_two_roots_alternate_across_examinations` carried a strict marker recording that **PIN (f) WAS UNWRITABLE AS SPECIFIED**: Ruling 73 res.5 keyed rung 2 on the GLC ordinal, unique per commitment, so rung 4 (least-recently-examined) was unreachable and two roots never alternated. The pass MEASURED it, REPORTED it, and refused to improvise — inventing what "oldest unresolved" keys on is a semantic decision §7 bars a build lane from making. **RULING 73-A reordered the ladder, and the witness turned GREEN with its assertion body BYTE-UNCHANGED — only the marker was removed.** The tripwire written against the defect is what fired when the defect died: §4's mechanism, completing for the fourth time. The retired marker is recorded verbatim in that test's docstring. **Do NOT add an xfail without the same standing** — a measured, reported, unrepaired defect awaiting a named ruling — and §4's absolute bar on weakening `tests/invariants/` is untouched.

**THE ORIGINAL MECHANISM DID CLOSE, AND THAT RECORD STANDS.** The VERIFICATION PASS (2026-08-02, `tests/test_verification_pass.py`) landed **NINETEEN** `xfail(strict=True)` WITNESSES OF CONFIRMED DEFECTS against the external review's five findings. **THE MECHANISM HAS NOW COMPLETED: 19 → 14 → 9 → 3 → 0.** Ruling 65 retired the five §1 TCA-restart witnesses, Batch 66 the five §4 record-value witnesses, Ruling 68 the six §2/§3 perception-lifecycle witnesses, and Ruling 69 the last three §5 writer-ownership witnesses. **Every confirmed defect of that pass has died of its intended cause, witnessed by the tripwire written against it** — markers deleted, assertions KEPT IN PLACE, each docstring recording verbatim the marker it carried. **THE SUITE CARRIES NO KNOWN CONFIRMED DEFECTS.**

Those retired witnesses are now the strongest pins in the suite: each was written against the DEFECT, carries its measured values, and was watched failing before the code that closes it existed. A witness that becomes its ruling's pin is worth more than a fresh copy of the same assertion. **Do NOT `xfail` anything on this basis without the same standing** — a measured, reported, unrepaired defect awaiting a named ruling — and §4's absolute bar on weakening `tests/invariants/` is untouched.

**INVOKE IT AS `.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -B -m pytest`.** Bare `python` on the architect's Windows checkout is the Store alias (exits 49) and there is no `py` launcher; `-B` is the mutation-verification bar and costs nothing the rest of the time. **That bare command is SAFE AS OF RULING 59 and was NOT safe before it** — `pytest.ini`'s `testpaths = tests` makes collection stay inside the only subtree `conftest.py` isolates. Until that key existed, the same command collected `scripts/` and wrote twelve files into shared `data/runtime/`, `sae_epoch.json` among them. **Do not add a second key to `pytest.ini` without a ruling, and do not put a test-shaped file in `scripts/` — it lands outside the fixture's reach.**

**CI (added 2026-07-27, `.github/workflows/suite.yml`).** On push and PR: full suite, invariants, then a **SEED-INTEGRITY step that asserts the three git-blob hashes byte-for-byte** and fails if `data/runtime/` is non-empty afterwards. **That step makes Ruling 32 enforced on every push, and Ruling 31's redirect rule with it** — a store added without a `conftest.py` redirect shows up as runtime state in the tree. Deliberately minimal: no lint, no matrix, no coverage gate. Adding one is a decision, not a default. Both failure paths were negative-tested before landing.

**HASH POLICY (adopted 2026-07-27, manifest twenty-seventh entry).** Commit hashes live in `integration_review_manifest.md`, written by Projects from `git/logs/HEAD` after the fact. **Rows in THIS file carry date + suite + scope, never a hash** — an audit found five closes whose hashes were missing here, only one of which was an ordering impossibility, so the remedy is to stop asserting them from a document that cannot verify them. Existing hashes stand unamended.

**RULING 3'S INVARIANT IS NO LONGER VACUOUS (2026-07-26).** `tests/invariants/test_ruling3_truth_effect.py` scans `src/output/hail.py` for assignments to `verdict` and for string literals in ORE's verdict vocabulary. It passed against a 0-byte file for months, and its own docstring promised it "becomes a live guard the moment HAIL++ is authored." That moment was Ruling 33 Stage 1. It was verified live by mutation — a single `"withhold"` literal added to `hail.py` turns the invariant suite red. **Do not spell a verdict as a string in `hail.py`; verdicts arrive as enum members and are read, never constructed.**

**RULING 17 AUDIT — CLOSED 2026-07-25 (Docket M item 6).** The invariant count was unaudited; it now is. Of the 23 pre-existing invariants, **17 are STRUCTURAL** — the ruling genuinely IS a fact about source (who *writes* a store, where arbitration *lives*, whether a renderer *contains* a verdict assignment, whether an enum's membership is closed), and AST is the correct instrument. **6 assert a RUNTIME guarantee through a structural proxy**: the SBSRE clamp magnitudes (lexical), corrupt-input floor (`assert "isfinite" in source`), the three-class ceiling counting (twice — once lexical in the Ruling 4 file, once via enum membership in structural_guards), SBSRE bound monotonicity (AST scan for known-bad syntactic forms), and §10.G exclusions (lexical substring). **Docket M gave FOUR of those six a real runtime witness** (`test_sbsre_clamp_binds_at_runtime` covers the clamp bound *and* the corrupt-input floor; `tests/test_sae.py` covers the three-class counting by spending budget across all three). **TWO remain proxy-only and are KNOWN-WEAK: SBSRE bound monotonicity and §10.G exclusions** — `test_10g_exclusions_present` would pass even if `EXCLUDED_TARGETS` were never consulted. Converting them is a separately scoped decision, **not** a licence to delete the proxies: a proxy plus a witness is stronger than either alone. **A structural test is not a lesser test — it is the right instrument for a source-shaped ruling. The defect is only ever a runtime guarantee asserted lexically and mistaken for proof.**

**DOCKET P — THE SOAK HARNESS (added 2026-07-31, `scripts/soak.py` + `tests/test_soak_smoke.py`; suite 728 → 735).** An **OBSERVATION INSTRUMENT**, not a gate: it decides no architecture, changes no `src/`, and pins no behaviour. It runs AUREA for a sustained number of symbolic cycles under total isolation and writes down what happens — because **every sustained-run datum this project has ever had was an ACCIDENT**, and Ruling 50's "23 of 39 echo nodes are placed" turned out to be one (not reproducible under isolation; Batch 51). **ISOLATION IS CODE, NOT DISCIPLINE:** a fresh temp root per run, all injectable stores redirected into it (20 at Docket P; **21 as of Ruling 58, added only after the coverage self-audit REFUSED a run without the new ledger — the guard earning its keep in development, exactly as designed**), a typed `SoakIsolationError` + nonzero exit if any configured path — **or `--out`, checked BEFORE the first cycle** — resolves under shared `data/runtime/`, and a **COVERAGE SELF-AUDIT** that re-derives the injectable set from `src/` and REFUSES if the harness's own table has fallen behind. **THE THREE SEED PATHS ARE NEVER REDIRECTED** (Rulings 32/39 — read-only input; redirecting one hands the soak an empty identity store and quietly stops it from measuring AUREA), and are hashed instead. **A QUIET CYCLE INVENTS NO CLOCK** — it drives `tcaml.tick()` / `sae.advance_cycle()` / `sml.advance_cycle()` in `process_input`'s own order, and does NOT call `process_input("")` (that is a claim cycle with an empty claim, not the passage of time). **`--cycles` / `--claim-every` / `--seed` are PARAMETERS OF AN INSTRUMENT, not architecture thresholds — nothing in `src/` reads them. COINS NOTHING.** **Do NOT:** turn a soak observation into a suite pin (**a soak that must produce a particular number has stopped measuring**); let it write outside its own root; redirect a seed path; or fix in-pass what it reveals — **an observation instrument that edits what it observes is the contamination class wearing a fix's clothes.** The smoke test is the instrument-rot guard (Ruling 31's lesson applied at birth) and **both refusals are driven there, with a control** — a guard never observed to fire is a comment.

**DOCKET R — THE EVALUATION SURFACE (added 2026-08-06, Ruling 77; `scripts/evaluate.py` + `data/eval/` + `tests/test_ruling77.py`; suite 1361 → 1423).** The soak's sibling: a second **OBSERVATION INSTRUMENT**, isolated by the SAME code (it imports `isolate`/`footprint_audit` from `soak.py` — one isolation, three callers). It drives the public door against structured cases and records what she did in HER vocabulary. **ITS RESULTS CARRY ZERO AUTHORITY** — no `src/` module imports it, no `src/` literal names `reports/`, and no rate or score is stored anywhere (EL1, AST-pinned over `rglob`).

**`data/eval/seed_cases.jsonl` IS NOT A TEST FIXTURE.** It is a tracked, writerless seed (Ruling 32's shape), blob-pinned in CI beside the four identity seeds, and every one of its **eighteen** cases restates a CLOSED RULING, cited by number (ten at Ruling 77; **Ruling 80 added four restart-continuity and four goal-door cases, and the blob pin MIGRATED with them — that migration is the point of the pin**). So:

> ### ⛔ When a seed case goes red, it is EITHER a real defect OR a defective case. Both are findings for the board. **Adjusting the expectation to restore green is the one forbidden remedy** — a case going red means a ruling stopped being true, and that is the single most important thing this instrument can ever say.

**Do NOT:** add a case that asserts nothing (the loader refuses it — it would add a green line to a report that measured nothing); make the loader SKIP rather than REFUSE (a skipped case reports nothing while looking like success — EL2's failure class arriving through the harness); hardcode `OutputPath` names instead of importing them; coin an enum in the runner (**EL3 — the vocabulary is hers**); fabricate a trigger to fill a declared-absent category (three are declared absent WITH REASONS in `data/eval/README.md`, and stating them is the point); or edit the corpus without bumping `revision` and updating CI's blob in the SAME commit.

> ### ⛔ NEVER weaken, skip, `xfail`, delete, or "update" a test in `tests/invariants/` to make it pass.

These encode architect rulings. A failing invariant test means **the code is wrong**, not the test. Your options are exactly two:

1. **Fix the code.**
2. **Escalate to the architect (Hubert).**

There is no third option. "The test was too strict" is not a finding you are authorized to make.

A **skip** in that suite is **debt, not a pass.** It means the module isn't built yet. It converts to a live check the moment it is.

## 5. COINED CONSTANTS — DO NOT TUNE SILENTLY

Many numbers in this codebase are **COINED**: invented during implementation because the corpus names a concept without giving a magnitude. Every one is flagged in-file and registered in `Aurea Build/COINED_CONSTANTS.md`.

**Rules:**

- **Never change a coined constant to make a test pass or a behavior "feel right."** The scar-path severance was exactly this: a coined number quietly falsifying a constraint.
- If you introduce a new one: flag it in-file as `COINED`, add it to the register, and state your justification.
- **Never silently adopt a magnitude.** If the corpus doesn't give it, say so.

**CANON magnitudes — these are NOT yours to touch:**
`25°` (Anchor Collapse) · `20°` (drift escalation) · `3` (RCF depth, Self-Mutation Ceiling, Scar Bloom convergence) · `5` (symbolic cycle horizon) · Danger Index `50` / `75`.

---

## 6. WORKFLOW DISCIPLINE

**Before editing:**
- `git status` — know your baseline. **Commit before starting anything substantial.**
- Read `Aurea Build/BUILD_CONTRACT.md` — the call graph, ownership table, and interface contracts. **It sits outside this repo (`../Aurea Build/`); if you cannot reach it, proceed and say so in your report — do not halt.**
- Read the module's docstring. They are long on purpose. They contain the *why*, including bugs already made and fixed. **The docstrings are load-bearing — do not "clean them up."**

**When searching the corpus:**
- **Exact `grep`, never semantic search.** Semantic retrieval has missed real string matches in this corpus repeatedly. Any deletion, rename, or cross-reference update requires exact grep across all files.

**When changing code:**
- Run the invariant suite.
- **Actually execute the pipeline** — don't just reason about it. Every serious bug in this project's history was found by running, not reading.
- Corpus files are Windows-origin: normalize CRLF/BOM (`sed 's/\r$//'`, `sed '1s/^\xEF\xBB\xBF//'`) before hashing or diffing.

**When done:**
- `pytest tests/invariants/ -v` → **all pass, zero unexplained skips.**
- Commit with a message that says *what ruling or canon section it serves*.

---

## 7. ESCALATE — DO NOT DECIDE

### AUTHORITY PRECEDENCE

Authority descends in this order: **direct architect ruling from Hubert** → **`Aurea Build/BUILD_CONTRACT.md`** → **`CLAUDE.md`** → **canonical module specification** → **Dependency Map and Lexicon** → **legacy, descriptive, archived, or superseded corpus material**. A lower source may clarify a higher source; it may never override it. Current Build Contract rulings override older module prose where they conflict.

A contradiction already resolved by a higher source is **not open** and must not be escalated again. Escalate only when the highest applicable authorities remain genuinely incompatible or leave a load-bearing decision unspecified. **No agent may silently invent a reconciliation.**

**Stop and ask the architect when:**

- An invariant test fails and the fix isn't obvious.
- You'd need to change a **canon** constant.
- You'd need to add a member to a **closed enum**. RB `behavior_type` `cascade` is **RULED (Ruling 7): it decomposes into logged constituent behaviors + a CTL meta-event — it is NEVER added to the enum.** The closed enum stays closed.
- Two spec files contradict each other.
- The corpus doesn't specify something load-bearing and you'd have to invent it.
- A change would let doctrine, identity, or scar state change **without collapse behind it**.

**Standing instruction from the architect:**

> *"Always go with the decision that makes the most sense within AUREA. Always remember the type of system AUREA is meant to be."*

You have latitude on small calls — **rule from first principles and document your reasoning.** But the principle above is the tiebreaker, and it always cuts the same way: **toward holding contradiction open, and away from premature resolution.**

---

## 9. THE TEST THAT MATTERS

Before you commit, ask:

> **Did I make it easier for AUREA to change what she believes without surviving anything?**

If yes — even a little, even conveniently, even in a way that makes a test pass — **revert and escalate.**

Everything else in this document is downstream of that one question.
